# Third-party notices

It includes the work of other people. This file names every package whose code is built into something It gives out or serves to a browser, with the license each is under. Each of them remains under its own license, as `LICENSE.md` says.

This file is written by `scripts/notices.mjs`, which builds each part and lists the packages that put code into it. Do not edit it by hand: run `npm run notices` after changing what a part depends on. The add-ons in `addons/` include no package at all, which the same script checks.

The standalone programs also hold the Bun runtime, which no build of a part shows. What Bun says it links and embeds is given below as Bun states it, with the words of every license that statement names, and after them the words for what Bun builds in and its statement does not name. Those are kept in `scripts/notices/`, and the script refuses to write this file where one of them is missing.

## What is in each part

The `it` program is what a person is given. The service that shows pages is part of it. It carries the other three parts inside it as they were built: it loads the functions into the backend, it serves the site, and it puts the script first in every page it shows. So a copy of the program holds every package in this table.

| Part | Packages built into it |
|---|---|
| The `it` program | `agent-base@7.1.4`, `asn1.js@5.4.1`, `bn.js@4.12.5`, `buffer-equal-constant-time@1.0.1`, `convex@1.46.0`, `debug@4.4.3`, `ecdsa-sig-formatter@1.0.11`, `fflate@0.8.3`, `http_ece@1.2.0`, `https-proxy-agent@7.0.6`, `inherits@2.0.4`, `jose@6.2.12`, `jwa@2.0.1`, `jws@4.0.1`, `minimalistic-assert@1.0.1`, `ms@2.1.3`, `qrcode-generator@2.0.4`, `safe-buffer@5.2.1`, `safer-buffer@2.1.2`, `web-push@3.6.7` |
| The backend's functions | `convex@1.46.0`, `jose@6.2.12` |
| The site | `convex@1.46.0`, `qrcode-generator@2.0.4`, `react-dom@19.3.0`, `react@19.3.0`, `scheduler@0.28.0`, `vite@7.3.6` |
| The script in every page | None |

## The runtime inside the standalone programs: Bun 1.3.11

The standalone programs are built with `bun build --compile`, which puts the `it` program's code inside a copy of the Bun runtime. The Bun they are built with reports itself as `1.3.11+af24e281e`: version 1.3.11, built from commit `af24e281ebacd6ac77c0f14b4206599cf4ae1c9f` of <https://github.com/oven-sh/bun>. The tag `bun-v1.3.11` of that repository is another commit, `a04817ce2b7f1a1e8b7cbf8af8f2c027ab072f1d`. Bun's statement, and the build files of Bun's that name the commits below, are the same at both.

What follows is Bun's own statement of its license and of what it links, `LICENSE.md` at that commit. Where the statement links to a branch of a library, which moves on, the link is given here as one to the commit that the library's text below is from.

```text
Bun itself is MIT-licensed.

## JavaScriptCore

Bun statically links JavaScriptCore (and WebKit) which is LGPL-2 licensed. WebCore files from WebKit are also licensed under LGPL2. Per LGPL2:

> (1) If you statically link against an LGPL’d library, you must also provide your application in an object (not necessarily source) format, so that a user has the opportunity to modify the library and relink the application.

You can find the patched version of WebKit used by Bun here: <https://github.com/oven-sh/webkit>. If you would like to relink Bun with changes:

- `git submodule update --init --recursive`
- `make jsc`
- `zig build`

This compiles JavaScriptCore, compiles Bun’s `.cpp` bindings for JavaScriptCore (which are the object files using JavaScriptCore) and outputs a new `bun` binary with your changes.

- Bun's JS transpiler, CSS lexer, and Node.js module resolver source code is a Zig port of [@evanw](https://github.com/evanw)’s [esbuild](https://github.com/evanw/esbuild) project.
- Credit to [@kipply](https://github.com/kipply) for the name "Bun"!
```

### The words of the licenses that statement names

Bun's statement names each license and gives the words of none. They follow, each as the library itself gives them at the commit that Bun's repository names for the library, and with each is said where it is from. Where Bun's repository names no commit for a library, or a library has no license file, that is said as well.

#### Bun

Bun's statement says: "Bun itself is MIT-licensed." Bun's repository at that commit has no license file but that statement at its root, and none of `LICENSE.md`, `README.md`, `package.json` and `docs/project/license.mdx` there has the words of the MIT license or says who holds the copyright in Bun. These are the words of the MIT license as mimalloc's `LICENSE` gives them at the commit named for it below, with the line that names mimalloc's copyright holders left out.

```text
MIT License

Permission is hereby granted, free of charge, to any person obtaining a copy
of this software and associated documentation files (the "Software"), to deal
in the Software without restriction, including without limitation the rights
to use, copy, modify, merge, publish, distribute, sublicense, and/or sell
copies of the Software, and to permit persons to whom the Software is
furnished to do so, subject to the following conditions:

The above copyright notice and this permission notice shall be included in all
copies or substantial portions of the Software.

THE SOFTWARE IS PROVIDED "AS IS", WITHOUT WARRANTY OF ANY KIND, EXPRESS OR
IMPLIED, INCLUDING BUT NOT LIMITED TO THE WARRANTIES OF MERCHANTABILITY,
FITNESS FOR A PARTICULAR PURPOSE AND NONINFRINGEMENT. IN NO EVENT SHALL THE
AUTHORS OR COPYRIGHT HOLDERS BE LIABLE FOR ANY CLAIM, DAMAGES OR OTHER
LIABILITY, WHETHER IN AN ACTION OF CONTRACT, TORT OR OTHERWISE, ARISING FROM,
OUT OF OR IN CONNECTION WITH THE SOFTWARE OR THE USE OR OTHER DEALINGS IN THE
SOFTWARE.
```

#### JavaScriptCore and WebKit

From <https://github.com/oven-sh/WebKit> at commit `00e825523d549a556d75985f486e4954af6ab8c7`, which `cmake/tools/SetupWebKit.cmake` in Bun's repository names. The folders of it that Bun's statement names, `Source/JavaScriptCore` and `Source/WebCore`, hold these license files.

#### boringssl

From <https://github.com/oven-sh/boringssl> at commit `4f4f5ef8ebc6e23cbf393428f0ab1b526773f7ac`, which `cmake/targets/BuildBoringSSL.cmake` in Bun's repository names.

From <https://github.com/oven-sh/tinycc> at commit `12882eee073cfe5c7621bcfadf679e1372d4537b`, which `cmake/targets/BuildTinyCC.cmake` in Bun's repository names.

#### uSockets

From <https://github.com/oven-sh/bun> at commit `af24e281ebacd6ac77c0f14b4206599cf4ae1c9f`. Bun keeps this library in its own repository, and the text is from there.

#### libicu

From <https://github.com/unicode-org/icu> at commit `ff3514f257ea10afe7e710e9f946f68d256704b1`. Bun's build files name no commit of ICU. On Linux and on Windows `cmake/targets/BuildBun.cmake` links the ICU that comes with the WebKit Bun is built with, and on macOS the system's own, and Bun's statement calls it version 72. Which commit of ICU that WebKit was built with is not established. The text is from the tag `release-72-1` of ICU's repository, which is this commit.

#### libbase64

From <https://github.com/aklomp/base64> at commit `bf058e571ac5002b75b03fed38e33ed4e8d45eff`. Bun's build files name no commit of this library, and Bun's repository keeps no source of it: what it keeps under `src/base64` is a file of its own and one compiled file, `neonbase64`, which names no version. So which commit of the library is linked is not established. The text is from the commit that the library's `master` branch stood at on 2026-10-04.

### The packages that statement says are embedded

Bun's statement names these packages under "Polyfills". The version of each is the one that `src/node-fallbacks/bun.lock` in Bun's repository names at that commit, and the text is from that version of the package as npm gives it out, whose checksum is the one the lock file has for it. For some of them, what Bun's repository builds from is a file of its own in `src/node-fallbacks` that holds a changed copy of the package's code, and the package's text is given all the same. The lock file also names the packages that these depend on, which the statement does not name and for which no text is given here.

#### assert 2.1.0

From the package `assert` at version 2.1.0, as npm gives it out: <https://registry.npmjs.org/assert/-/assert-2.1.0.tgz>.

#### stream-browserify

`src/node-fallbacks/bun.lock` in Bun's repository names no version of this package at that commit, and `src/node-fallbacks/package.json` there does not ask for it, so no text of it is given. `src/node-fallbacks/stream.js` there takes the package `readable-stream` in its place, which the lock file has at 4.7.0.

### What Bun builds in or keeps, and its statement does not name

Bun's build files name these libraries, or Bun's repository or its WebKit keeps them, and Bun's statement names none of them. The statement gives no list to hold these to, so this one is of what Bun's build files register and of what its repository and its WebKit keep beside the libraries above, and it may not be all there is.

#### ls-hpack

From <https://github.com/litespeedtech/ls-hpack> at commit `8905c024b6d052f083a3d11d0a169b3c2735c8a1`, which `cmake/targets/BuildLshpack.cmake` in Bun's repository names. Bun's build file has it built with the xxHash it keeps under `deps/xxhash`, and on Windows with the `queue.h` it keeps under `compat/queue`. Neither has a license file of its own, and what is given for each is the comment its source file opens with.

#### SQLite

From <https://github.com/oven-sh/bun> at commit `af24e281ebacd6ac77c0f14b4206599cf4ae1c9f`. Bun keeps SQLite in its own repository as one file. Its build files build that file in on every system but macOS, where `cmake/Options.cmake` has the system's own SQLite used. The file says that it is made of the source files of SQLite version 3.51.2. SQLite has no license file there, and this is the comment that the first of those source files opens with.

`src/bun.js/bindings/sqlite/sqlite3.c`:

```text
/*
** 2001 September 15
**
** The author disclaims copyright to this source code.  In place of
** a legal notice, here is a blessing:
**
**    May you do good and not evil.
**    May you find forgiveness for yourself and forgive others.
**    May you share freely, never taking more than you give.
**
*************************************************************************
** Internal interface definitions for SQLite.
**
*/
```

#### zig-clap

From <https://github.com/oven-sh/bun> at commit `af24e281ebacd6ac77c0f14b4206599cf4ae1c9f`. Bun keeps this library in its own repository, and the text is from there. `src/deps/zig-clap/gyro.zzz` there gives its version as 0.3.0 and its source as <https://github.com/Hejsil/zig-clap>.


#### What WebKit's WTF carries

From <https://github.com/oven-sh/WebKit> at commit `00e825523d549a556d75985f486e4954af6ab8c7`, which `cmake/tools/SetupWebKit.cmake` in Bun's repository names. These are the license files that WebKit keeps in `Source/WTF`, beside the code of others that it carries there.

## The backend program, which is fetched and not included

It runs Convex's backend program, `convex-local-backend`, on the person's own machine. That program is in nothing It gives out. The `it` program fetches it the first time It is set up on a machine, from the release `precompiled-2026-09-28-5c7cb5b` at <https://github.com/get-convex/convex-backend/releases>, or from a mirror of those releases where the person names one with `IT_BACKEND_RELEASES`, and keeps what it fetched only if it matches a SHA-256 checksum written in It's own source.

The program is Convex's work and stays under Convex's terms. In the description of its own API, which it carries, it names its license as `LicenseRef-FSL-1.1-Apache-2.0`. The terms themselves are in its repository, <https://github.com/get-convex/convex-backend>.

## Typefaces the site carries

The pictures on the first screen of the site are drawn in typefaces that the site serves from `/fonts/`, as files converted to WOFF2. Each remains under its own license, which is given here in full.

### Inter and Inter Display 4.000

By The Inter Project Authors (Rasmus Andersson). License: SIL Open Font License 1.1. Served as: `display-600.woff2`, `display-700.woff2`, `display-800.woff2`, `text-400.woff2`, `text-500.woff2`, `text-600.woff2`, `text-700.woff2`, which hold Latin subsets of seven weights.
